People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I just… that thought scared me enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, somewhat. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It started after my sister died unexpectedly. That was about a month ago now.\n\nI mean, I’m sure people go through things like this and manage, so I don’t want to make too much of it… but that’s when I noticed I stopped feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to measure “most of the day.” I have had quite a few low stretches, yes… but I don’t want to overstate it and say it’s every day if that’s not quite right. It comes and goes some.\n\nDoctor: When you feel low, is it mostly when you’re thinking about your sister or reminded of her?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, thinking about my sister definitely brings it up, of course… but I haven’t really kept track of whether that’s the main time. Sometimes I just feel low and then wonder if it’s connected, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still go to things. I make myself show up for book club, and I went to the dance night, so it’s not like I’m just doing nothing.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels sort of… distant. Like I’m behind a pane of glass, if that makes sense. And even things I usually really enjoy, like my garden or cooking something for the neighbors, they don’t give me that little lift they used to. I hope I’m explaining that alright.\n\nDoctor: When you suddenly feel low, does it usually happen after something reminds you of your sister?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say “usually,” no. Sometimes it is after something reminds me of her, and that makes sense to me. But other times it seems to come over me without a clear reason, or I only connect it to her afterward. I may not be noticing the pattern very well, though.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have there been at least several days when you felt down nearly all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say nearly all day. I just… that thought scared me enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, somewhat. I don’t know if I’d call it terrible, exactly, but I haven’t felt like myself lately. Just kind of low. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It started after my sister died unexpectedly. That was about a month ago now.\n\nI mean, I’m sure people go through things like this and manage, so I don’t want to make too much of it… but that’s when I noticed I stopped feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to measure “most of the day.” I have had quite a few low stretches, yes… but I don’t want to overstate it and say it’s every day if that’s not quite right. It comes and goes some.\n\nDoctor: When you feel low, is it mostly when you’re thinking about your sister or reminded of her?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, thinking about my sister definitely brings it up, of course… but I haven’t really kept track of whether that’s the main time. Sometimes I just feel low and then wonder if it’s connected, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still go to things. I make myself show up for book club, and I went to the dance night, so it’s not like I’m just doing nothing.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels sort of… distant. Like I’m behind a pane of glass, if that makes sense. And even things I usually really enjoy, like my garden or cooking something for the neighbors, they don’t give me that little lift they used to. I hope I’m explaining that alright.\n\nDoctor: When you suddenly feel low, does it usually happen after something reminds you of your sister?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say “usually,” no. Sometimes it is after something reminds me of her, and that makes sense to me. But other times it seems to come over me without a clear reason, or I only connect it to her afterward. I may not be noticing the pattern very well, though.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have there been at least several days when you felt down nearly all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say nearly all day. There have been some days where I felt pretty down for a good part of the day, but I don’t know if it counts the way you mean. I may not be judging it very clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt low on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe it’s close, but I don’t want to overstate it. There have definitely been a number of low days, but I’ve also had days where I could get through things and seem fairly normal. \n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’ve felt low on several days, but whether it’s more days than not, I’m not certain. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel most down, are you usually thinking about your sister?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s usually, no. Sometimes she does come to mind when I’m really down, and then it sort of feels connected. But other times I’m just… low, and I can’t quite tell what I’m thinking about, if that makes sense. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been worrying a lot about many different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not a lot about many different things. I mean, I do have ordinary worries, I suppose, but I haven’t really noticed it being sort of constant or without a reason. I may be missing something, but I wouldn’t say that’s been a big part of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this low mood is mainly part of grieving your sister’s death?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, her death is part of it sometimes, of course, and I wouldn’t want to make it sound like it has nothing to do with her. But I can’t honestly say it’s mainly that every time.\n\nSometimes I feel low and I don’t quite know what to attach it to. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I almost feel a little embarrassed saying it, because I don’t want to make it sound overly dramatic. But there have been some mornings where I wake up and think it might be easier if I just… didn’t wake up at all.\n\nI don’t have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. I just… that thought scared me enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, somewhat. I don’t know if I’d call it terrible, exactly, but I haven’t felt like myself lately. Just kind of low. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It started after my sister died unexpectedly. That was about a month ago now.\n\nI mean, I’m sure people go through things like this and manage, so I don’t want to make too much of it… but that’s when I noticed I stopped feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to measure “most of the day.” I have had quite a few low stretches, yes… but I don’t want to overstate it and say it’s every day if that’s not quite right. It comes and goes some.\n\nDoctor: When you feel low, is it mostly when you’re thinking about your sister or reminded of her?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, thinking about my sister definitely brings it up, of course… but I haven’t really kept track of whether that’s the main time. Sometimes I just feel low and then wonder if it’s connected, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still go to things. I make myself show up for book club, and I went to the dance night, so it’s not like I’m just doing nothing.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels sort of… distant. Like I’m behind a pane of glass, if that makes sense. And even things I usually really enjoy, like my garden or cooking something for the neighbors, they don’t give me that little lift they used to. I hope I’m explaining that alright.\n\nDoctor: When you suddenly feel low, does it usually happen after something reminds you of your sister?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say “usually,” no. Sometimes it is after something reminds me of her, and that makes sense to me. But other times it seems to come over me without a clear reason, or I only connect it to her afterward. I may not be noticing the pattern very well, though.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have there been at least several days when you felt down nearly all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say nearly all day. There have been some days where I felt pretty down for a good part of the day, but I don’t know if it counts the way you mean. I may not be judging it very clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt low on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe it’s close, but I don’t want to overstate it. There have definitely been a number of low days, but I’ve also had days where I could get through things and seem fairly normal. \n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’ve felt low on several days, but whether it’s more days than not, I’m not certain. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel most down, are you usually thinking about your sister?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s usually, no. Sometimes she does come to mind when I’m really down, and then it sort of feels connected. But other times I’m just… low, and I can’t quite tell what I’m thinking about, if that makes sense. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been worrying a lot about many different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not a lot about many different things. I mean, I do have ordinary worries, I suppose, but I haven’t really noticed it being sort of constant or without a reason. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It started after my sister died unexpectedly. That was about a month ago now.\n\nI mean, I’m sure people go through things like this and manage, so I don’t want to make too much of it… but that’s when I noticed I stopped feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to measure “most of the day.” I have had quite a few low stretches, yes… but I don’t want to overstate it and say it’s every day if that’s not quite right. It comes and goes some.\n\nDoctor: When you feel low, is it mostly when you’re thinking about your sister or reminded of her?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, thinking about my sister definitely brings it up, of course… but I haven’t really kept track of whether that’s the main time. Sometimes I just feel low and then wonder if it’s connected, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still go to things. I make myself show up for book club, and I went to the dance night, so it’s not like I’m just doing nothing.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels sort of… distant. Like I’m behind a pane of glass, if that makes sense. And even things I usually really enjoy, like my garden or cooking something for the neighbors, they don’t give me that little lift they used to. I hope I’m explaining that alright.\n\nDoctor: When you suddenly feel low, does it usually happen after something reminds you of your sister?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say “usually,” no. Sometimes it is after something reminds me of her, and that makes sense to me. But other times it seems to come over me without a clear reason, or I only connect it to her afterward. I may not be noticing the pattern very well, though.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have there been at least several days when you felt down nearly all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say nearly all day. I may not be judging it very clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt low on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel most down, are you usually thinking about your sister?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s usually, no. Sometimes she does come to mind when I’m really down, and then it sort of feels connected. But other times I’m just… low, and I can’t quite tell what I’m thinking about, if that makes sense. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been worrying a lot about many different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not a lot about many different things. It comes and goes some.\n\nDoctor: When you feel low, is it mostly when you’re thinking about your sister or reminded of her?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, thinking about my sister definitely brings it up, of course… but I haven’t really kept track of whether that’s the main time. Sometimes I just feel low and then wonder if it’s connected, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still go to things. I make myself show up for book club, and I went to the dance night, so it’s not like I’m just doing nothing.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels sort of… distant. Like I’m behind a pane of glass, if that makes sense. And even things I usually really enjoy, like my garden or cooking something for the neighbors, they don’t give me that little lift they used to. I hope I’m explaining that alright.\n\nDoctor: When you suddenly feel low, does it usually happen after something reminds you of your sister?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say “usually,” no. Sometimes it is after something reminds me of her, and that makes sense to me. But other times it seems to come over me without a clear reason, or I only connect it to her afterward. I may not be noticing the pattern very well, though.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have there been at least several days when you felt down nearly all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say nearly all day. There have been some days where I felt pretty down for a good part of the day, but I don’t know if it counts the way you mean. I may not be judging it very clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt low on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe it’s close, but I don’t want to overstate it. There have definitely been a number of low days, but I’ve also had days where I could get through things and seem fairly normal. \n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’ve felt low on several days, but whether it’s more days than not, I’m not certain. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel most down, are you usually thinking about your sister?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s usually, no. Sometimes she does come to mind when I’m really down, and then it sort of feels connected. But other times I’m just… low, and I can’t quite tell what I’m thinking about, if that makes sense. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been worrying a lot about many different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not a lot about many different things. I mean, I do have ordinary worries, I suppose, but I haven’t really noticed it being sort of constant or without a reason. I may be missing something, but I wouldn’t say that’s been a big part of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this low mood is mainly part of grieving your sister’s death?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, her death is part of it sometimes, of course, and I wouldn’t want to make it sound like it has nothing to do with her. But I can’t honestly say it’s mainly that every time.\n\nSometimes I feel low and I don’t quite know what to attach it to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you ever gone through a period like this where you felt similarly low?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that I had, not in quite this way. I’ve had sad spells, like anyone, and difficult times, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same as this.\n\nI hope that’s answering what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you felt down or depressed more days than not for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not for that long, anyway. Before she died, I had ups and downs, but I wouldn’t say I was down more days than not for two years. I hope that’s the kind of distinction you mean.\n\nDoctor: Did these low feelings begin only after your sister died?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. It really started after my sister died unexpectedly, about a month ago. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s when I noticed these low feelings beginning.\n\nDoctor: Since these low feelings began, have they made it hard for you to manage your usual daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m still trying to keep up, but I did have to ask another volunteer to cover parts of the Juneteenth budgeting workshop because I just couldn’t manage the preparation the way I usually would. And I’ve been saying no to neighbors and friends more than I normally do.\n\nI hope that’s not making it sound worse than it is, but… yes, it has made some things harder.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I don’t know if I’d call it terrible, exactly, but I haven’t felt like myself lately. Just kind of low. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It started after my sister died unexpectedly. That was about a month ago now.\n\nI mean, I’m sure people go through things like this and manage, so I don’t want to make too much of it… but that’s when I noticed I stopped feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to measure “most of the day.” I have had quite a few low stretches, yes… but I don’t want to overstate it and say it’s every day if that’s not quite right. It comes and goes some.\n\nDoctor: When you feel low, is it mostly when you’re thinking about your sister or reminded of her?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, thinking about my sister definitely brings it up, of course… but I haven’t really kept track of whether that’s the main time. Sometimes I just feel low and then wonder if it’s connected, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still go to things. I make myself show up for book club, and I went to the dance night, so it’s not like I’m just doing nothing.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels sort of… distant. Like I’m behind a pane of glass, if that makes sense. And even things I usually really enjoy, like my garden or cooking something for the neighbors, they don’t give me that little lift they used to. I hope I’m explaining that alright.\n\nDoctor: When you suddenly feel low, does it usually happen after something reminds you of your sister?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say “usually,” no. Sometimes it is after something reminds me of her, and that makes sense to me. But other times it seems to come over me without a clear reason, or I only connect it to her afterward. I may not be noticing the pattern very well, though.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have there been at least several days when you felt down nearly all day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say nearly all day. There have been some days where I felt pretty down for a good part of the day, but I don’t know if it counts the way you mean. I may not be judging it very clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt low on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe it’s close, but I don’t want to overstate it. There have definitely been a number of low days, but I’ve also had days where I could get through things and seem fairly normal. \n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’ve felt low on several days, but whether it’s more days than not, I’m not certain. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel most down, are you usually thinking about your sister?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s usually, no. Sometimes she does come to mind when I’m really down, and then it sort of feels connected. But other times I’m just… low, and I can’t quite tell what I’m thinking about, if that makes sense. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been worrying a lot about many different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not a lot about many different things. I mean, I do have ordinary worries, I suppose, but I haven’t really noticed it being sort of constant or without a reason. I may be missing something, but I wouldn’t say that’s been a big part of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this low mood is mainly part of grieving your sister’s death?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, her death is part of it sometimes, of course, and I wouldn’t want to make it sound like it has nothing to do with her. But I can’t honestly say it’s mainly that every time.\n\nSometimes I feel low and I don’t quite know what to attach it to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you ever gone through a period like this where you felt similarly low?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that I had, not in quite this way. I’ve had sad spells, like anyone, and difficult times, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same as this.\n\nI hope that’s answering what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you felt down or depressed more days than not for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not for that long, anyway. Before she died, I had ups and downs, but I wouldn’t say I was down more days than not for two years. I hope that’s the kind of distinction you mean.\n\nDoctor: Did these low feelings begin only after your sister died?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. It really started after my sister died unexpectedly, about a month ago. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s when I noticed these low feelings beginning.\n\nDoctor: Since these low feelings began, have they made it hard for you to manage your usual daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m still trying to keep up, but I did have to ask another volunteer to cover parts of the Juneteenth budgeting workshop because I just couldn’t manage the preparation the way I usually would. And I’ve been saying no to neighbors and friends more than I normally do.\n\nI hope that’s not making it sound worse than it is, but… yes, it has made some things harder.\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day or lost interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Not that I can clearly remember, no. I mean, I’ve had sad stretches like anyone, but before my sister died I don’t think there was a two-week period where I felt depressed nearly every day or lost interest in most things. If there was, it wasn’t something that stood out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or blaming yourself a lot?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I don’t know if I’d call it worthless exactly, but I do find myself going over those last weeks with my sister again and again. I keep thinking I should have called more, or listened better, or done something more useful.\n\nPart of me knows I probably couldn’t have changed what happened, but… I still blame myself more than I should, I think.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I may not be judging it very clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt low on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think… maybe it’s close, but I don’t want to overstate it. There have definitely been a number of low days, but I’ve also had days where I could get through things and seem fairly normal. \n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’ve felt low on several days, but whether it’s more days than not, I’m not certain. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel most down, are you usually thinking about your sister?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s usually, no. Sometimes she does come to mind when I’m really down, and then it sort of feels connected. But other times I’m just… low, and I can’t quite tell what I’m thinking about, if that makes sense. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been worrying a lot about many different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not a lot about many different things. I mean, I do have ordinary worries, I suppose, but I haven’t really noticed it being sort of constant or without a reason. I may be missing something, but I wouldn’t say that’s been a big part of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this low mood is mainly part of grieving your sister’s death?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, her death is part of it sometimes, of course, and I wouldn’t want to make it sound like it has nothing to do with her. But I can’t honestly say it’s mainly that every time.\n\nSometimes I feel low and I don’t quite know what to attach it to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you ever gone through a period like this where you felt similarly low?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that I had, not in quite this way. I’ve had sad spells, like anyone, and difficult times, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same as this.\n\nI hope that’s answering what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you felt down or depressed more days than not for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not for that long, anyway. Before she died, I had ups and downs, but I wouldn’t say I was down more days than not for two years. I hope that’s the kind of distinction you mean.\n\nDoctor: Did these low feelings begin only after your sister died?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. It really started after my sister died unexpectedly, about a month ago. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s when I noticed these low feelings beginning.\n\nDoctor: Since these low feelings began, have they made it hard for you to manage your usual daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m still trying to keep up, but I did have to ask another volunteer to cover parts of the Juneteenth budgeting workshop because I just couldn’t manage the preparation the way I usually would. And I’ve been saying no to neighbors and friends more than I normally do.\n\nI hope that’s not making it sound worse than it is, but… yes, it has made some things harder.\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day or lost interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Not that I can clearly remember, no. I mean, I’ve had sad stretches like anyone, but before my sister died I don’t think there was a two-week period where I felt depressed nearly every day or lost interest in most things. If there was, it wasn’t something that stood out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or blaming yourself a lot?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I don’t know if I’d call it worthless exactly, but I do find myself going over those last weeks with my sister again and again. I keep thinking I should have called more, or listened better, or done something more useful.\n\nPart of me knows I probably couldn’t have changed what happened, but… I still blame myself more than I should, I think.\n\nDoctor: Since these low feelings began, has there been a stretch of two weeks when you felt down nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s been about a month now, since my sister died unexpectedly. I keep wanting to say it’s not every single moment, but yes, I’ve felt down nearly every day during that time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. There have definitely been a number of low days, but I’ve also had days where I could get through things and seem fairly normal. \n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’ve felt low on several days, but whether it’s more days than not, I’m not certain. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel most down, are you usually thinking about your sister?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s usually, no. Sometimes she does come to mind when I’m really down, and then it sort of feels connected. But other times I’m just… low, and I can’t quite tell what I’m thinking about, if that makes sense. I don’t want to give you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been worrying a lot about many different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not a lot about many different things. I mean, I do have ordinary worries, I suppose, but I haven’t really noticed it being sort of constant or without a reason. I may be missing something, but I wouldn’t say that’s been a big part of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this low mood is mainly part of grieving your sister’s death?\n\nPatient: I’m not entirely sure. I mean, her death is part of it sometimes, of course, and I wouldn’t want to make it sound like it has nothing to do with her. But I can’t honestly say it’s mainly that every time.\n\nSometimes I feel low and I don’t quite know what to attach it to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you ever gone through a period like this where you felt similarly low?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that I had, not in quite this way. I’ve had sad spells, like anyone, and difficult times, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same as this.\n\nI hope that’s answering what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you felt down or depressed more days than not for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not for that long, anyway. Before she died, I had ups and downs, but I wouldn’t say I was down more days than not for two years. I hope that’s the kind of distinction you mean.\n\nDoctor: Did these low feelings begin only after your sister died?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. It really started after my sister died unexpectedly, about a month ago. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s when I noticed these low feelings beginning.\n\nDoctor: Since these low feelings began, have they made it hard for you to manage your usual daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m still trying to keep up, but I did have to ask another volunteer to cover parts of the Juneteenth budgeting workshop because I just couldn’t manage the preparation the way I usually would. And I’ve been saying no to neighbors and friends more than I normally do.\n\nI hope that’s not making it sound worse than it is, but… yes, it has made some things harder.\n\nDoctor: Before your sister died, had you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day or lost interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Not that I can clearly remember, no. I mean, I’ve had sad stretches like anyone, but before my sister died I don’t think there was a two-week period where I felt depressed nearly every day or lost interest in most things. If there was, it wasn’t something that stood out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or blaming yourself a lot?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I don’t know if I’d call it worthless exactly, but I do find myself going over those last weeks with my sister again and again. I keep thinking I should have called more, or listened better, or done something more useful.\n\nPart of me knows I probably couldn’t have changed what happened, but… I still blame myself more than I should, I think.\n\nDoctor: Since these low feelings began, has there been a stretch of two weeks when you felt down nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s been about a month now, since my sister died unexpectedly. I keep wanting to say it’s not every single moment, but yes, I’ve felt down nearly every day during that time.\n\nDoctor: When you feel most down lately, is it usually because something has reminded you of your sister?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. Sometimes it is, yes—like if I see something of hers or think of something we used to talk about. But I don’t know that I’ve noticed a clear pattern every time. I suppose a lot of it circles back to her, if that makes sense."
